Right now your app is running, but the predictions are still low confidence and wrong (e.g. it should detect acris_blanchardi but instead thinks it’s litoria_ewingii at ~27%). That means:

✅ The inference pipeline is working (audio → MFCC → CNN → predictions).

❌ But the model itself isn’t recognizing species correctly.

This can happen for a few reasons:

Training vs inference mismatch: MFCC extraction parameters (hop length, window size, normalization) might differ between training and app.

Window segmentation: Even with multi-window or flux-based call picking, the frog call might not be isolated properly.

Label noise or imbalance: If acris_blanchardi had too few good-quality examples, the model underfit that class.

Overfitting: The model performed on training data, but real WAV inference has small shifts → confidence collapses.

Wrong checkpoint/label_map alignment: If the labels are misaligned with the model checkpoint, species indices don’t match.

Thought for 14s

Awesome — here’s a tiny, no‑Streamlit sanity checker that runs your trained CNN on one WAV and prints super‑detailed diagnostics. It uses the same MFCC settings (sr=16k, n_mfcc=13, n_fft=1024, hop=160, per‑coef z‑norm, frames=100) and the same FrogCNN head layout we used for the good results.

Save this as FrogSoundProject/src/debug_infer.py and run it.

# FrogSoundProject/src/debug_infer.py
import os, json, argparse, numpy as np
import librosa
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

DEFAULT_MODEL = os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","frog_cnn_best.pt")
DEFAULT_LABELS = os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","label_map.json")

# ---- Model that matches your good checkpoint ----
class FrogCNN(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, n_classes, in_ch=1):
        super().__init__()
        self.features = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Conv2d(in_ch, 16, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(16), nn.ReLU(), nn.MaxPool2d((1,2)),
            nn.Conv2d(16, 32, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(32), nn.ReLU(), nn.MaxPool2d((1,2)),
            nn.Conv2d(32, 64, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(64), nn.ReLU(),
            nn.AdaptiveMaxPool2d((13, 1)),  # -> 64*13*1 (=832) features
        )
        # Indices mirror the checkpoint (linear layers at 2 and 5)
        self.classifier = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Flatten(),          # 0
            nn.Identity(),         # 1
            nn.Linear(64*13*1,256),# 2
            nn.ReLU(),             # 3
            nn.Identity(),         # 4
            nn.Linear(256,n_classes) # 5
        )
    def forward(self, x):
        return self.classifier(self.features(x))

def load_label_map(path):
    with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        lm = json.load(f)
    # list
    if isinstance(lm, list) and all(isinstance(x,str) for x in lm):
        return lm
    # {"classes":[...]}
    if isinstance(lm, dict) and "classes" in lm:
        c = lm["classes"]
        if all(isinstance(x,str) for x in c): return c
        if all(isinstance(x,dict) and "name" in x for x in c): return [x["name"] for x in c]
    # name->idx
    if isinstance(lm, dict) and all(isinstance(k,str) for k in lm) and all(isinstance(v,int) for v in lm.values()):
        return [k for k,_ in sorted(lm.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1])]
    # idx->name (keys str or int)
    if isinstance(lm, dict) and all(isinstance(v,str) for v in lm.values()):
        try:
            kv = [(int(k),v) for k,v in lm.items()]; kv.sort(key=lambda x:x[0]); return [v for _,v in kv]
        except: pass
    raise ValueError("Unrecognized label_map format.")

def wav_to_mfcc_13(wav_path, sr_target=16000):
    y, sr = librosa.load(wav_path, sr=sr_target, mono=True)
    mfcc = librosa.feature.mfcc(y=y, sr=sr, n_mfcc=13, n_fft=1024, hop_length=160, center=True) # (13,T)
    return mfcc.astype(np.float32)

def pad_crop(a, T):
    c,t = a.shape
    if t < T:
        a = np.concatenate([a, np.zeros((c, T-t), dtype=a.dtype)], axis=1)
    elif t > T:
        a = a[:, :T]
    return a

def print_stats(tag, arr):
    arr = arr.astype(np.float64)
    print(f"{tag}: shape={arr.shape}  mean={arr.mean():.4f}  std={arr.std():.4f}  min={arr.min():.4f}  max={arr.max():.4f}")

def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Sanity-check single WAV against frog model.")
    ap.add_argument("--wav", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--model", default=DEFAULT_MODEL)
    ap.add_argument("--label-map", default=DEFAULT_LABELS)
    ap.add_argument("--frames", type=int, default=100)
    ap.add_argument("--normalize", action="store_true", help="Per-coef z-norm (recommended).")
    ap.add_argument("--device", default="cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    print("=== Debug Infer ===")
    print(f"WAV: {args.wav}")
    print(f"Model: {args.model}")
    print(f"Labels: {args.label_map}")
    print(f"Frames target: {args.frames} | Device: {args.device}")

    ckpt = torch.load(args.model, map_location="cpu")
    classes = None
    state_dict = None
    frames_ckpt = None
    if isinstance(ckpt, dict):
        if "classes" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["classes"], list):
            classes = ckpt["classes"]
            print(f"Loaded classes from checkpoint: {len(classes)}")
        if "frames" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["frames"], int):
            frames_ckpt = ckpt["frames"]
            print(f"Checkpoint frames: {frames_ckpt}")
        if "model_state" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["model_state"], dict):
            state_dict = ckpt["model_state"]
        elif "state_dict" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["state_dict"], dict):
            state_dict = ckpt["state_dict"]

    if classes is None:
        classes = load_label_map(args.label_map)
        print(f"Loaded classes from label_map: {len(classes)}")

    T = frames_ckpt if frames_ckpt is not None else args.frames
    n_classes = len(classes)

    model = FrogCNN(n_classes=n_classes)
    if state_dict is None:
        # maybe raw state_dict
        state_dict = ckpt if isinstance(ckpt, dict) else ckpt
    missing, unexpected = model.load_state_dict(state_dict, strict=False)
    if missing or unexpected:
        print(f"load_state_dict warnings -> missing: {list(missing)} | unexpected: {list(unexpected)}")
    device = torch.device(args.device)
    model.to(device).eval()

    # Audio -> MFCC -> (optional) z-norm -> pad/crop -> tensor
    mfcc = wav_to_mfcc_13(args.wav, sr_target=16000)
    print_stats("MFCC raw", mfcc)
    if args.normalize:
        mu = mfcc.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)
        sd = mfcc.std(axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-6
        mfcc = (mfcc - mu) / sd
        print_stats("MFCC z-norm", mfcc)
        print("Per-coef mean (first 5):", np.round(mu[:5,0],4))
        print("Per-coef std  (first 5):", np.round(sd[:5,0],4))
    mfcc = pad_crop(mfcc, T)
    print_stats(f"MFCC after pad/crop(T={T})", mfcc)

    x = torch.from_numpy(mfcc[None, None, ...]).to(device)  # (1,1,13,T)
    with torch.no_grad():
        logits = model(x)
        probs = F.softmax(logits, dim=1).cpu().numpy()[0]
    # Top-10
    idxs = np.argsort(-probs)[:10]
    print("\nTop-10 predictions:")
    for rank, i in enumerate(idxs, 1):
        print(f"{rank:2d}. {classes[i]:<28} {probs[i]*100:6.2f}%")

    # Quick sanity markers
    top_i = int(idxs[0])
    print("\nSUMMARY")
    print(f"Top class: {classes[top_i]} | confidence: {probs[top_i]*100:.2f}%")
    print(f"Sum(probs)={probs.sum():.6f} (should be 1.0)")
    print(f"argmax index: {top_i} / {n_classes}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

(Deliberately omit --normalize.)

Why this matters

Your model was trained on already‑normalized .npy MFCCs. When you add a second per‑inference z‑norm (--normalize), it distorts the distribution → soft predictions.

One window (T=100) rarely captures a whole call; multi‑window mean stabilizes things.

If you still see low confidence

Try --strategy logit_mean (averages logits then softmax; often sharper):

... --multi 12 --strategy logit_mean


Bump coverage: --multi 20.

If the WAV is stereo or odd SR, the scripts downmix/resample for you; no action needed.

strategy = "mean" (or "logit_mean" if you prefer)

no normalization flag for inference

uncertainty slider is fine

Thought for 57s

You’re seeing confident but wrong guesses because the MFCCs we extract at inference time don’t perfectly match the MFCCs you trained on. Let’s fix that in two pragmatic steps that also make things snappy:

#1 — Prefer the processed .npy (exact features used in training)
I’m giving you a drop‑in infer_wav.py that, when given a WAV, first looks for a matching processed .npy in FrogSoundProject/data/processed/**. If it finds one, it uses that (no librosa at all), which guarantees feature parity with training. If it can’t find a match, it falls back to librosa MFCC.

#2 — (Optional but recommended) Global MFCC standardization
Add a one‑time stats file (models/mfcc_stats.json) computed from your processed set, so that when we must fall back to librosa we can standardize MFCCs the same way your dataset “looks” globally. This usually sharpens predictions and avoids the super‑flat softmax you saw.

A. Replace src/infer_wav.py with this

Save this exact file over FrogSoundProject/src/infer_wav.py.

# FrogSoundProject/src/infer_wav.py
import os, json, argparse, glob, numpy as np
import librosa
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

# ---------------- Model (matches your trained checkpoint layout) ----------------
class FrogCNN(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, n_classes, in_ch=1):
        super().__init__()
        self.features = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Conv2d(in_ch, 16, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(16), nn.ReLU(), nn.MaxPool2d((1,2)),
            nn.Conv2d(16, 32, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(32), nn.ReLU(), nn.MaxPool2d((1,2)),
            nn.Conv2d(32, 64, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(64), nn.ReLU(),
            nn.AdaptiveMaxPool2d((13, 1)),    # -> 64*13*1 = 832
        )
        # Indices chosen so the checkpoint lines up: linear at 2 and 5
        self.classifier = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Flatten(),            # 0
            nn.Identity(),           # 1
            nn.Linear(64*13*1, 256), # 2
            nn.ReLU(),               # 3
            nn.Identity(),           # 4
            nn.Linear(256, n_classes) # 5
        )
    def forward(self, x):
        return self.classifier(self.features(x))

# ---------------- Utilities ----------------
def pad_or_crop(a, T):
    c, t = a.shape
    if t < T:
        pad = np.zeros((c, T - t), dtype=a.dtype)
        a = np.concatenate([a, pad], axis=1)
    elif t > T:
        a = a[:, :T]
    return a

def try_find_processed_npy(wav_path, processed_root):
    """
    Try to locate a processed .npy that corresponds to this WAV by stem.
    Example: .../raw/.../acris_blanchardi_obs3026.wav -> look for any
    processed/**/acris_blanchardi_obs3026*.npy
    """
    stem = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(wav_path))[0]
    pattern = os.path.join(processed_root, "**", f"{stem}*.npy")
    hits = glob.glob(pattern, recursive=True)
    return hits[0] if hits else None

def load_mfcc_stats(stats_path):
    if stats_path and os.path.exists(stats_path):
        with open(stats_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            js = json.load(f)
        mean = np.asarray(js.get("mean"), dtype=np.float32)  # shape (13,)
        std  = np.asarray(js.get("std"),  dtype=np.float32)
        if mean.shape == (13,) and std.shape == (13,) and np.all(std > 0):
            return mean, std
    return None, None

def librosa_mfcc_13(wav_path, sr_target=16000, n_fft=1024, hop_length=160, win_length=None, center=True, htk=False, norm='ortho'):
    y, sr = librosa.load(wav_path, sr=sr_target, mono=True)
    mfcc = librosa.feature.mfcc(
        y=y, sr=sr, n_mfcc=13,
        n_fft=n_fft, hop_length=hop_length, win_length=win_length,
        center=center, htk=htk, norm=norm
    )  # (13, T)
    return mfcc.astype(np.float32)

def load_label_map_any(path, fallback=None):
    cls = None
    if path and os.path.exists(path):
        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            lm = json.load(f)
        if isinstance(lm, list) and all(isinstance(x, str) for x in lm):
            cls = lm
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and "classes" in lm and isinstance(lm["classes"], list):
            if all(isinstance(x, str) for x in lm["classes"]):
                cls = lm["classes"]
            elif all(isinstance(x, dict) and "name" in x for x in lm["classes"]):
                cls = [x["name"] for x in lm["classes"]]
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and all(isinstance(k,str) for k in lm.keys()) and all(isinstance(v,int) for v in lm.values()):
            size = max(lm.values())+1
            cls = [None]*size
            for name, idx in lm.items():
                if 0 <= idx < size: cls[idx] = name
            if any(x is None for x in cls):
                cls = [k for k,_ in sorted(lm.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1])]
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and all(isinstance(v,str) for v in lm.values()):
            try:
                kv = [(int(k),v) for k,v in lm.items()]
                kv.sort(key=lambda x:x[0]); cls = [v for _,v in kv]
            except Exception:
                pass
    return cls if cls is not None else (fallback or [])

# ---------------- Inference core ----------------
def infer_single(mfcc, model, frames, device, multi=1, strategy="mean"):
    """
    mfcc: (13, T_total) float32, already standardized if desired
    Returns probs (n_classes,) averaged/aggregated across windows.
    """
    T = frames
    if multi <= 1 or mfcc.shape[1] <= T:
        win = pad_or_crop(mfcc, T)[None, None, ...]  # (1,1,13,T)
        with torch.no_grad():
            logits = model(torch.from_numpy(win).to(device))
            probs = F.softmax(logits, dim=1).cpu().numpy()[0]
        return probs

    # multi windows evenly spaced
    total = mfcc.shape[1]
    idxs = np.linspace(0, max(total - T, 0), num=multi, dtype=int)
    agg_logits = None
    agg_probs  = None
    for s in idxs:
        sub = mfcc[:, s:s+T]
        sub = pad_or_crop(sub, T)[None, None, ...]
        with torch.no_grad():
            logits = model(torch.from_numpy(sub).to(device))
            probs  = F.softmax(logits, dim=1)

        if strategy in ("logit_mean", "logit-mean"):
            agg_logits = probs.new_zeros_like(logits) if agg_logits is None else agg_logits
            agg_logits += logits
        elif strategy == "max":
            agg_probs = probs if agg_probs is None else torch.maximum(agg_probs, probs)
        else:  # mean
            agg_probs = probs if agg_probs is None else agg_probs + probs

    if strategy in ("logit_mean", "logit-mean"):
        probs = F.softmax(agg_logits / len(idxs), dim=1).cpu().numpy()[0]
    elif strategy == "max":
        probs = agg_probs.cpu().numpy()[0]
    else:
        probs = (agg_probs / len(idxs)).cpu().numpy()[0]
    return probs

# ---------------- Main ----------------
def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Infer frog species from a WAV file.")
    ap.add_argument("--wav", required=True, help="Path to audio .wav")
    ap.add_argument("--model", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","frog_cnn_best.pt"))
    ap.add_argument("--label-map", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","label_map.json"))
    ap.add_argument("--frames", type=int, default=100)
    ap.add_argument("--topk", type=int, default=5)
    ap.add_argument("--uncertain-threshold", type=float, default=None, help="If top prob < thr, print UNCERTAIN")
    ap.add_argument("--normalize", action="store_true", help="(fallback path only) per-coef z-norm per clip")
    ap.add_argument("--multi", type=int, default=12, help="# windows")
    ap.add_argument("--strategy", choices=["mean","max","logit_mean","logit-mean"], default="mean")
    ap.add_argument("--device", default="cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
    ap.add_argument("--processed-root", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","data","processed"))
    ap.add_argument("--mfcc-stats", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","mfcc_stats.json"),
                    help="Optional global MFCC stats (mean/std per coef) for fallback librosa path")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    print("=== Frog Inference ===")
    print(f"WAV:      {args.wav}")
    print(f"Model:    {args.model}")
    print(f"Labels:   {args.label_map}")
    print(f"Frames:   {args.frames} | TopK: {args.topk}")

    # Load checkpoint
    ckpt = torch.load(args.model, map_location="cpu")
    classes = None
    target_frames = args.frames
    state_dict = None

    if isinstance(ckpt, dict):
        if "classes" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["classes"], list):
            classes = ckpt["classes"]; print(f"Loaded {len(classes)} classes from checkpoint.")
        if "frames" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["frames"], int):
            target_frames = ckpt["frames"]; print(f"Using target frames from checkpoint: {target_frames}")
        if "model_state" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["model_state"], dict):
            state_dict = ckpt["model_state"]
        elif "state_dict" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["state_dict"], dict):
            state_dict = ckpt["state_dict"]

    # fallback label map if needed
    if classes is None:
        classes = load_label_map_any(args.label_map)
        print(f"Loaded {len(classes)} classes from label_map.json")
    n_classes = len(classes)

    # Build model + load weights
    model = FrogCNN(n_classes=n_classes)
    if state_dict is None:
        # maybe ckpt is raw state_dict
        if isinstance(ckpt, dict):
            tensor_only = {k:v for k,v in ckpt.items() if isinstance(v, torch.Tensor)}
            state_dict = tensor_only if tensor_only else ckpt
        else:
            state_dict = ckpt
    missing, unexpected = model.load_state_dict(state_dict, strict=False)
    if missing or unexpected:
        print(f"⚠️ load_state_dict warnings — missing: {list(missing)}, unexpected: {list(unexpected)}")

    device = torch.device(args.device); model.to(device); model.eval()

    # ---------- Path 1: exact processed features if available ----------
    npy_match = try_find_processed_npy(args.wav, args.processed_root)
    if npy_match and os.path.exists(npy_match):
        a = np.load(npy_match).astype(np.float32)
        if a.ndim == 3: a = a.squeeze(-1)  # (13,T)
        if a.shape[0] != 13:
            raise ValueError(f"Processed file has unexpected shape: {a.shape}")
        probs = infer_single(a, model, target_frames, device, multi=args.multi, strategy=args.strategy)

    else:
        # ---------- Path 2: fallback librosa MFCC + (optional) global standardization ----------
        mfcc = librosa_mfcc_13(args.wav, sr_target=16000, n_fft=1024, hop_length=160, win_length=None, center=True, htk=False, norm='ortho')
        # Option A: per-clip z-norm if user asked
        if args.normalize:
            mfcc = (mfcc - mfcc.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)) / (mfcc.std(axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-6)
        # Option B: global stats if available (recommended)
        mean, std = load_mfcc_stats(args.mfcc_stats)
        if mean is not None and std is not None:
            # broadcast (13,1)
            mfcc = (mfcc - mean[:,None]) / (std[:,None] + 1e-6)

        probs = infer_single(mfcc, model, target_frames, device, multi=args.multi, strategy=args.strategy)

    # Top-K
    topk = min(args.topk, n_classes)
    idxs = np.argsort(-probs)[:topk]
    print("\nTop predictions:")
    for i in idxs:
        print(f"  {classes[i]:<28} {probs[i]*100:6.2f}%")

    if args.uncertain_threshold is not None:
        top = probs[idxs[0]] * 100.0
        if top < args.uncertain_threshold:
            print(f"\n🤔 UNCERTAIN — top confidence {top:.2f}% (< {args.uncertain_threshold:.0f}%)")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

How to use it

For dataset WAVs (like your iNat files), this will automatically find and use the matching processed .npy, which should fix the “wrong but confident” predictions.

For arbitrary WAVs, it falls back to librosa MFCC + optional global standardization.

Run (fast path, multi-window mean, no per‑clip normalize):

python FrogSoundProject\src\infer_wav.py `
  --wav "C:\path\to\your.wav" `
  --model "FrogSoundProject\models\frog_cnn_best.pt" `
  --label-map "FrogSoundProject\models\label_map.json" `
  --frames 100 `
  --topk 5 `
  --multi 12 `
  --strategy mean


If you want the UNCERTAIN message:

... --uncertain-threshold 70

B. (Optional) Create global MFCC stats once

This helps when you classify new WAVs (not in your processed set).

Save this as FrogSoundProject/src/make_mfcc_stats.py:

import os, json, numpy as np
from glob import glob

PROC_DIR = os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","data","processed")
OUT = os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","mfcc_stats.json")

def main():
    sums = np.zeros(13, dtype=np.float64)
    sqs  = np.zeros(13, dtype=np.float64)
    nframes = 0

    npys = glob(os.path.join(PROC_DIR, "*", "*.npy"))
    if not npys:
        print("No processed .npy files found.")
        return

    for i, fp in enumerate(npys, 1):
        a = np.load(fp)
        if a.ndim == 3: a = a.squeeze(-1)
        if a.shape[0] != 13: continue
        sums += a.sum(axis=1)
        sqs  += (a**2).sum(axis=1)
        nframes += a.shape[1]
        if i % 1000 == 0:
            print(f"Processed {i}/{len(npys)} files...")

    mean = sums / max(nframes,1)
    var  = sqs / max(nframes,1) - mean**2
    std  = np.sqrt(np.maximum(var, 1e-12))

    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(OUT), exist_ok=True)
    with open(OUT, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump({"mean": mean.tolist(), "std": std.tolist()}, f, indent=2)
    print(f"Saved MFCC stats -> {OUT}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


Run it once:

python FrogSoundProject\src\make_mfcc_stats.py


This creates models/mfcc_stats.json. The new infer_wav.py will auto‑use it when it can’t find a matching processed .npy.

Why this fixes your problem

Your training/eval used the processed MFCCs. Feeding the model different MFCCs (even small param differences) can shift scores or point to the wrong species. Using the matching .npy ensures perfect feature parity.

When processed .npy doesn’t exist (new audio), global standardization helps align distributions so your model sees something closer to what it saw during training.

If you want, I can also wire the Streamlit app to prefer processed .npy exactly like this (and keep the UI minimal).

Nice — you’ve got the MFCC stats built and the new infer script running. The big thing we still need is visibility into what features were used and a way to force the exact processed .npy that was seen during training. If the script fell back to librosa for that iNat WAV, that can still skew predictions (even after global stats).

Here’s a drop-in infer_wav.py that adds:

Prints the feature source (Processed vs. Librosa) and the path used.

--npy <path> to force a specific processed feature file.

--processed-only to error out if a matching processed .npy can’t be found.

Optional focusing on the loudest windows before aggregation: --focus loudest --focus-k 6.

Replace your file with this one and retry.

FrogSoundProject/src/infer_wav.py (full)
import os, json, argparse, glob, numpy as np
import librosa
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

# ---------------- Model (matches your trained checkpoint layout) ----------------
class FrogCNN(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, n_classes, in_ch=1):
        super().__init__()
        self.features = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Conv2d(in_ch, 16, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(16), nn.ReLU(), nn.MaxPool2d((1,2)),
            nn.Conv2d(16, 32, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(32), nn.ReLU(), nn.MaxPool2d((1,2)),
            nn.Conv2d(32, 64, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(64), nn.ReLU(),
            nn.AdaptiveMaxPool2d((13, 1)),    # -> 64*13*1 = 832
        )
        # Indices chosen so the checkpoint lines up: linear at 2 and 5
        self.classifier = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Flatten(),            # 0
            nn.Identity(),           # 1
            nn.Linear(64*13*1, 256), # 2
            nn.ReLU(),               # 3
            nn.Identity(),           # 4
            nn.Linear(256, n_classes) # 5
        )
    def forward(self, x):
        return self.classifier(self.features(x))

# ---------------- Utilities ----------------
def pad_or_crop(a, T):
    c, t = a.shape
    if t < T:
        pad = np.zeros((c, T - t), dtype=a.dtype)
        a = np.concatenate([a, pad], axis=1)
    elif t > T:
        a = a[:, :T]
    return a

def try_find_processed_npy(wav_path, processed_root):
    stem = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(wav_path))[0]
    pattern = os.path.join(processed_root, "**", f"{stem}*.npy")
    hits = glob.glob(pattern, recursive=True)
    return hits[0] if hits else None

def load_mfcc_stats(stats_path):
    if stats_path and os.path.exists(stats_path):
        with open(stats_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            js = json.load(f)
        mean = np.asarray(js.get("mean"), dtype=np.float32)
        std  = np.asarray(js.get("std"),  dtype=np.float32)
        if mean.shape == (13,) and std.shape == (13,) and np.all(std > 0):
            return mean, std
    return None, None

def librosa_mfcc_13(wav_path, sr_target=16000, n_fft=1024, hop_length=160, win_length=None, center=True, htk=False, norm='ortho'):
    y, sr = librosa.load(wav_path, sr=sr_target, mono=True)
    mfcc = librosa.feature.mfcc(
        y=y, sr=sr, n_mfcc=13,
        n_fft=n_fft, hop_length=hop_length, win_length=win_length,
        center=center, htk=htk, norm=norm
    )  # (13, T)
    return mfcc.astype(np.float32)

def load_label_map_any(path, fallback=None):
    cls = None
    if path and os.path.exists(path):
        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            lm = json.load(f)
        if isinstance(lm, list) and all(isinstance(x, str) for x in lm):
            cls = lm
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and "classes" in lm and isinstance(lm["classes"], list):
            if all(isinstance(x, str) for x in lm["classes"]):
                cls = lm["classes"]
            elif all(isinstance(x, dict) and "name" in x for x in lm["classes"]):
                cls = [x["name"] for x in lm["classes"]]
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and all(isinstance(k,str) for k in lm.keys()) and all(isinstance(v,int) for v in lm.values()):
            size = max(lm.values())+1
            cls = [None]*size
            for name, idx in lm.items():
                if 0 <= idx < size: cls[idx] = name
            if any(x is None for x in cls):
                cls = [k for k,_ in sorted(lm.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1])]
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and all(isinstance(v,str) for v in lm.values()):
            try:
                kv = [(int(k),v) for k,v in lm.items()]
                kv.sort(key=lambda x:x[0]); cls = [v for _,v in kv]
            except Exception:
                pass
    return cls if cls is not None else (fallback or [])

def window_rms(mfcc_win):
    # mfcc_win: (13,T) -> simple RMS over time & coefs
    return float(np.sqrt(np.mean(mfcc_win**2)))

# ---------------- Inference core ----------------
def infer_multi_windows(mfcc, model, frames, device, multi, strategy, focus, focus_k):
    T = frames
    total = mfcc.shape[1]
    if total <= T or multi <= 1:
        win = pad_or_crop(mfcc, T)[None, None, ...]
        with torch.no_grad():
            logits = model(torch.from_numpy(win).to(device))
            probs = F.softmax(logits, dim=1).cpu().numpy()[0]
        return probs

    # evenly spaced starts
    starts = np.linspace(0, max(total - T, 0), num=multi, dtype=int)
    windows = []
    for s in starts:
        sub = mfcc[:, s:s+T]
        sub = pad_or_crop(sub, T)
        windows.append(sub)

    if focus == "loudest":
        # score windows by RMS; keep top-K
        scores = [window_rms(w) for w in windows]
        keep_idx = np.argsort(scores)[-min(focus_k, len(windows)):]
        windows = [windows[i] for i in keep_idx]

    agg_logits = None
    agg_probs  = None
    for sub in windows:
        x = sub[None, None, ...]
        with torch.no_grad():
            logits = model(torch.from_numpy(x).to(device))
            probs  = F.softmax(logits, dim=1)

        if strategy in ("logit_mean", "logit-mean"):
            agg_logits = probs.new_zeros_like(logits) if agg_logits is None else agg_logits
            agg_logits += logits
        elif strategy == "max":
            agg_probs = probs if agg_probs is None else torch.maximum(agg_probs, probs)
        else:  # mean
            agg_probs = probs if agg_probs is None else agg_probs + probs

    if strategy in ("logit_mean", "logit-mean"):
        probs = F.softmax(agg_logits / len(windows), dim=1).cpu().numpy()[0]
    elif strategy == "max":
        probs = agg_probs.cpu().numpy()[0]
    else:
        probs = (agg_probs / len(windows)).cpu().numpy()[0]
    return probs

# ---------------- Main ----------------
def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Infer frog species from a WAV file.")
    ap.add_argument("--wav", required=True, help="Path to audio .wav")
    ap.add_argument("--npy", help="(Optional) Path to a processed MFCC .npy to force using training features")
    ap.add_argument("--processed-only", action="store_true", help="Require a processed .npy; error if none found")
    ap.add_argument("--model", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","frog_cnn_best.pt"))
    ap.add_argument("--label-map", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","label_map.json"))
    ap.add_argument("--frames", type=int, default=100)
    ap.add_argument("--topk", type=int, default=5)
    ap.add_argument("--uncertain-threshold", type=float, default=None, help="If top prob < thr, print 'UNCERTAIN'")
    ap.add_argument("--normalize", action="store_true", help="(fallback path only) per-coef z-norm per clip")
    ap.add_argument("--multi", type=int, default=12, help="# windows")
    ap.add_argument("--strategy", choices=["mean","max","logit_mean","logit-mean"], default="mean")
    ap.add_argument("--focus", choices=["none","loudest"], default="none", help="Pick loudest windows before aggregation")
    ap.add_argument("--focus-k", type=int, default=6, help="How many windows to keep if --focus loudest")
    ap.add_argument("--device", default="cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
    ap.add_argument("--processed-root", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","data","processed"))
    ap.add_argument("--mfcc-stats", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","mfcc_stats.json"),
                    help="Optional global MFCC stats (mean/std per coef) for fallback librosa path")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    print("=== Frog Inference ===")
    print(f"WAV:      {args.wav}")
    print(f"Model:    {args.model}")
    print(f"Labels:   {args.label_map}")
    print(f"Frames:   {args.frames} | TopK: {args.topk}")

    # Load checkpoint
    ckpt = torch.load(args.model, map_location="cpu")
    classes = None
    target_frames = args.frames
    state_dict = None

    if isinstance(ckpt, dict):
        if "classes" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["classes"], list):
            classes = ckpt["classes"]; print(f"Loaded {len(classes)} classes from checkpoint.")
        if "frames" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["frames"], int):
            target_frames = ckpt["frames"]; print(f"Using target frames from checkpoint: {target_frames}")
        if "model_state" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["model_state"], dict):
            state_dict = ckpt["model_state"]
        elif "state_dict" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["state_dict"], dict):
            state_dict = ckpt["state_dict"]

    if classes is None:
        classes = load_label_map_any(args.label_map)
        print(f"Loaded {len(classes)} classes from label_map.json")
    n_classes = len(classes)

    # Build model + load weights
    model = FrogCNN(n_classes=n_classes)
    if state_dict is None:
        if isinstance(ckpt, dict):
            tensor_only = {k:v for k,v in ckpt.items() if isinstance(v, torch.Tensor)}
            state_dict = tensor_only if tensor_only else ckpt
        else:
            state_dict = ckpt
    missing, unexpected = model.load_state_dict(state_dict, strict=False)
    if missing or unexpected:
        print(f"⚠️ load_state_dict warnings — missing: {list(missing)}, unexpected: {list(unexpected)}")

    device = torch.device(args.device); model.to(device); model.eval()

    # ---------------- Feature source selection ----------------
    used_source = None
    used_path = None

    # 1) explicit --npy
    if args.npy:
        if not os.path.exists(args.npy):
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"--npy not found: {args.npy}")
        a = np.load(args.npy).astype(np.float32)
        if a.ndim == 3: a = a.squeeze(-1)
        if a.shape[0] != 13:
            raise ValueError(f"--npy has unexpected shape {a.shape}, expected (13,T) or (13,T,1)")
        used_source = "Processed (.npy, explicit)"
        used_path = args.npy
        mfcc = a

    else:
        # 2) auto-find processed
        npy_match = try_find_processed_npy(args.wav, args.processed_root)
        if npy_match and os.path.exists(npy_match):
            a = np.load(npy_match).astype(np.float32)
            if a.ndim == 3: a = a.squeeze(-1)
            if a.shape[0] != 13:
                raise ValueError(f"Processed file has unexpected shape: {a.shape}")
            used_source = "Processed (.npy, auto)"
            used_path = npy_match
            mfcc = a
        else:
            if args.processed-only:
                raise FileNotFoundError("No matching processed .npy found and --processed-only was set.")
            # 3) fallback librosa
            mfcc = librosa_mfcc_13(args.wav, sr_target=16000, n_fft=1024, hop_length=160, win_length=None, center=True, htk=False, norm='ortho')
            if args.normalize:
                mfcc = (mfcc - mfcc.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)) / (mfcc.std(axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-6)
            mean, std = load_mfcc_stats(args.mfcc_stats)
            if mean is not None and std is not None:
                mfcc = (mfcc - mean[:,None]) / (std[:,None] + 1e-6)
            used_source = "Librosa MFCC (fallback)"
            used_path = args.wav

    print(f"Feature source: {used_source}")
    print(f"Feature path:   {used_path}")

    # ---------------- Inference ----------------
    probs = infer_multi_windows(
        mfcc, model, target_frames, device,
        multi=max(1, args.multi), strategy=args.strategy,
        focus=args.focus, focus_k=args.focus_k
    )

    # Top-K
    topk = min(args.topk, n_classes)
    idxs = np.argsort(-probs)[:topk]
    print("\nTop predictions:")
    for i in idxs:
        print(f"  {classes[i]:<32} {probs[i]*100:6.2f}%")

    if args.uncertain_threshold is not None:
        top = probs[idxs[0]] * 100.0
        if top < args.uncertain_threshold:
            print(f"\n🤔 UNCERTAIN — top confidence {top:.2f}% (< {args.uncertain_threshold:.0f}%)")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

You’re running an older infer_wav.py (it doesn’t know --npy, --focus, or --focus-k). Replace it with the version below, then run the commands I put after it (with a real .npy path, not C:\...\).

FrogSoundProject/src/infer_wav.py (drop-in, full)
import os, json, argparse, glob, numpy as np
import librosa
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

# ---------------- Model (matches your trained checkpoint layout) ----------------
class FrogCNN(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, n_classes, in_ch=1):
        super().__init__()
        self.features = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Conv2d(in_ch, 16, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(16), nn.ReLU(), nn.MaxPool2d((1,2)),
            nn.Conv2d(16, 32, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(32), nn.ReLU(), nn.MaxPool2d((1,2)),
            nn.Conv2d(32, 64, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(64), nn.ReLU(),
            nn.AdaptiveMaxPool2d((13, 1)),    # -> 64*13*1 = 832
        )
        # Indices chosen so the checkpoint lines up: linear at 2 and 5
        self.classifier = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Flatten(),            # 0
            nn.Identity(),           # 1
            nn.Linear(64*13*1, 256), # 2
            nn.ReLU(),               # 3
            nn.Identity(),           # 4
            nn.Linear(256, n_classes) # 5
        )
    def forward(self, x):
        return self.classifier(self.features(x))

# ---------------- Utilities ----------------
def pad_or_crop(a, T):
    c, t = a.shape
    if t < T:
        pad = np.zeros((c, T - t), dtype=a.dtype)
        a = np.concatenate([a, pad], axis=1)
    elif t > T:
        a = a[:, :T]
    return a

def try_find_processed_npy(wav_path, processed_root):
    stem = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(wav_path))[0]
    pattern = os.path.join(processed_root, "**", f"{stem}*.npy")
    hits = glob.glob(pattern, recursive=True)
    return hits[0] if hits else None

def load_mfcc_stats(stats_path):
    if stats_path and os.path.exists(stats_path):
        with open(stats_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            js = json.load(f)
        mean = np.asarray(js.get("mean"), dtype=np.float32)
        std  = np.asarray(js.get("std"),  dtype=np.float32)
        if mean.shape == (13,) and std.shape == (13,) and np.all(std > 0):
            return mean, std
    return None, None

def librosa_mfcc_13(wav_path, sr_target=16000, n_fft=1024, hop_length=160, win_length=None, center=True, htk=False, norm='ortho'):
    y, sr = librosa.load(wav_path, sr=sr_target, mono=True)
    mfcc = librosa.feature.mfcc(
        y=y, sr=sr, n_mfcc=13,
        n_fft=n_fft, hop_length=hop_length, win_length=win_length,
        center=center, htk=htk, norm=norm
    )  # (13, T)
    return mfcc.astype(np.float32)

def load_label_map_any(path, fallback=None):
    cls = None
    if path and os.path.exists(path):
        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            lm = json.load(f)
        if isinstance(lm, list) and all(isinstance(x, str) for x in lm):
            cls = lm
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and "classes" in lm and isinstance(lm["classes"], list):
            if all(isinstance(x, str) for x in lm["classes"]):
                cls = lm["classes"]
            elif all(isinstance(x, dict) and "name" in x for x in lm["classes"]):
                cls = [x["name"] for x in lm["classes"]]
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and all(isinstance(k,str) for k in lm.keys()) and all(isinstance(v,int) for v in lm.values()):
            size = max(lm.values())+1
            cls = [None]*size
            for name, idx in lm.items():
                if 0 <= idx < size: cls[idx] = name
            if any(x is None for x in cls):
                cls = [k for k,_ in sorted(lm.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1])]
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and all(isinstance(v,str) for v in lm.values()):
            try:
                kv = [(int(k),v) for k,v in lm.items()]
                kv.sort(key=lambda x:x[0]); cls = [v for _,v in kv]
            except Exception:
                pass
    return cls if cls is not None else (fallback or [])

def window_rms(mfcc_win):
    return float(np.sqrt(np.mean(mfcc_win**2)))

# ---------------- Inference core ----------------
def infer_multi_windows(mfcc, model, frames, device, multi, strategy, focus, focus_k):
    T = frames
    total = mfcc.shape[1]
    if total <= T or multi <= 1:
        win = pad_or_crop(mfcc, T)[None, None, ...]
        with torch.no_grad():
            logits = model(torch.from_numpy(win).to(device))
            probs = F.softmax(logits, dim=1).cpu().numpy()[0]
        return probs

    # evenly spaced starts
    starts = np.linspace(0, max(total - T, 0), num=multi, dtype=int)
    windows = []
    for s in starts:
        sub = mfcc[:, s:s+T]
        sub = pad_or_crop(sub, T)
        windows.append(sub)

    if focus == "loudest":
        scores = [window_rms(w) for w in windows]
        keep_idx = np.argsort(scores)[-min(focus_k, len(windows)):]
        windows = [windows[i] for i in keep_idx]

    agg_logits = None
    agg_probs  = None
    for sub in windows:
        x = sub[None, None, ...]
        with torch.no_grad():
            logits = model(torch.from_numpy(x).to(device))
            probs  = F.softmax(logits, dim=1)

        if strategy in ("logit_mean", "logit-mean"):
            agg_logits = probs.new_zeros_like(logits) if agg_logits is None else agg_logits
            agg_logits += logits
        elif strategy == "max":
            agg_probs = probs if agg_probs is None else torch.maximum(agg_probs, probs)
        else:  # mean
            agg_probs = probs if agg_probs is None else agg_probs + probs

    if strategy in ("logit_mean", "logit-mean"):
        probs = F.softmax(agg_logits / len(windows), dim=1).cpu().numpy()[0]
    elif strategy == "max":
        probs = agg_probs.cpu().numpy()[0]
    else:
        probs = (agg_probs / len(windows)).cpu().numpy()[0]
    return probs

# ---------------- Main ----------------
def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Infer frog species from a WAV file.")
    ap.add_argument("--wav", required=True, help="Path to audio .wav")
    ap.add_argument("--npy", help="(Optional) Path to a processed MFCC .npy to force using training features")
    ap.add_argument("--processed-only", action="store_true", help="Require a processed .npy; error if none found")
    ap.add_argument("--model", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","frog_cnn_best.pt"))
    ap.add_argument("--label-map", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","label_map.json"))
    ap.add_argument("--frames", type=int, default=100)
    ap.add_argument("--topk", type=int, default=5)
    ap.add_argument("--uncertain-threshold", type=float, default=None, help="If top prob < thr, print 'UNCERTAIN'")
    ap.add_argument("--normalize", action="store_true", help="(fallback path only) per-coef z-norm per clip")
    ap.add_argument("--multi", type=int, default=12, help="# windows to evaluate")
    ap.add_argument("--strategy", choices=["mean","max","logit_mean","logit-mean"], default="mean", help="Aggregate across windows")
    ap.add_argument("--focus", choices=["none","loudest"], default="none", help="Optionally keep loudest windows only")
    ap.add_argument("--focus-k", type=int, default=6, help="How many windows to keep if --focus loudest")
    ap.add_argument("--device", default="cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
    ap.add_argument("--processed-root", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","data","processed"))
    ap.add_argument("--mfcc-stats", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","mfcc_stats.json"),
                    help="Optional global MFCC stats (mean/std per coef) for fallback librosa path")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    print("=== Frog Inference ===")
    print(f"WAV:      {args.wav}")
    print(f"Model:    {args.model}")
    print(f"Labels:   {args.label_map}")
    print(f"Frames:   {args.frames} | TopK: {args.topk}")

    # Load checkpoint
    ckpt = torch.load(args.model, map_location="cpu")
    classes = None
    target_frames = args.frames
    state_dict = None

    if isinstance(ckpt, dict):
        if "classes" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["classes"], list):
            classes = ckpt["classes"]; print(f"Loaded {len(classes)} classes from checkpoint.")
        if "frames" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["frames"], int):
            target_frames = ckpt["frames"]; print(f"Using target frames from checkpoint: {target_frames}")
        if "model_state" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["model_state"], dict):
            state_dict = ckpt["model_state"]
        elif "state_dict" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["state_dict"], dict):
            state_dict = ckpt["state_dict"]

    if classes is None:
        classes = load_label_map_any(args.label_map)
        print(f"Loaded {len(classes)} classes from label_map.json")
    n_classes = len(classes)

    # Build model + load weights
    model = FrogCNN(n_classes=n_classes)
    if state_dict is None:
        if isinstance(ckpt, dict):
            tensor_only = {k:v for k,v in ckpt.items() if isinstance(v, torch.Tensor)}
            state_dict = tensor_only if tensor_only else ckpt
        else:
            state_dict = ckpt
    missing, unexpected = model.load_state_dict(state_dict, strict=False)
    if missing or unexpected:
        print(f"⚠️ load_state_dict warnings — missing: {list(missing)}, unexpected: {list(unexpected)}")

    device = torch.device(args.device); model.to(device); model.eval()

    # ---------------- Feature source selection ----------------
    used_source = None
    used_path = None

    if args.npy:
        if not os.path.exists(args.npy):
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"--npy not found: {args.npy}")
        a = np.load(args.npy).astype(np.float32)
        if a.ndim == 3: a = a.squeeze(-1)
        if a.shape[0] != 13:
            raise ValueError(f"--npy has unexpected shape {a.shape}, expected (13,T) or (13,T,1)")
        used_source = "Processed (.npy, explicit)"; used_path = args.npy
        mfcc = a
    else:
        npy_match = try_find_processed_npy(args.wav, args.processed_root)
        if npy_match and os.path.exists(npy_match):
            a = np.load(npy_match).astype(np.float32)
            if a.ndim == 3: a = a.squeeze(-1)
            if a.shape[0] != 13:
                raise ValueError(f"Processed file has unexpected shape: {a.shape}")
            used_source = "Processed (.npy, auto)"; used_path = npy_match
            mfcc = a
        else:
            if args.processed_only:
                raise FileNotFoundError("No matching processed .npy found and --processed-only was set.")
            mfcc = librosa_mfcc_13(args.wav, sr_target=16000, n_fft=1024, hop_length=160, win_length=None, center=True, htk=False, norm='ortho')
            if args.normalize:
                mfcc = (mfcc - mfcc.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)) / (mfcc.std(axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-6)
            mean, std = load_mfcc_stats(args.mfcc_stats)
            if mean is not None and std is not None:
                mfcc = (mfcc - mean[:,None]) / (std[:,None] + 1e-6)
            used_source = "Librosa MFCC (fallback)"; used_path = args.wav

    print(f"Feature source: {used_source}")
    print(f"Feature path:   {used_path}")

    # ---------------- Inference ----------------
    probs = infer_multi_windows(
        mfcc, model, target_frames, device,
        multi=max(1, args.multi), strategy=args.strategy,
        focus=args.focus, focus_k=args.focus_k
    )

    # Top-K
    topk = min(args.topk, n_classes)
    idxs = np.argsort(-probs)[:topk]
    print("\nTop predictions:")
    for i in idxs:
        print(f"  {classes[i]:<32} {probs[i]*100:6.2f}%")

    if args.uncertain_threshold is not None:
        top = probs[idxs[0]] * 100.0
        if top < args.uncertain_threshold:
            print(f"\n🤔 UNCERTAIN — top confidence {top:.2f}% (< {args.uncertain_threshold:.0f}%)")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

You’re still running the older infer_wav.py. Let’s overwrite it with the version that supports --npy, --focus, and --focus-k, then verify.

$dst = "FrogSoundProject\src\infer_wav.py"
@'
import os, json, argparse, glob, numpy as np
import librosa
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

# ---------------- Model ----------------
class FrogCNN(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, n_classes, in_ch=1):
        super().__init__()
        self.features = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Conv2d(in_ch, 16, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(16), nn.ReLU(), nn.MaxPool2d((1,2)),
            nn.Conv2d(16, 32, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(32), nn.ReLU(), nn.MaxPool2d((1,2)),
            nn.Conv2d(32, 64, 3, padding=1), nn.BatchNorm2d(64), nn.ReLU(),
            nn.AdaptiveMaxPool2d((13, 1)),
        )
        # keep linear at indices 2 and 5 to match your checkpoint
        self.classifier = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Flatten(),            # 0
            nn.Identity(),           # 1
            nn.Linear(64*13*1, 256), # 2
            nn.ReLU(),               # 3
            nn.Identity(),           # 4
            nn.Linear(256, n_classes) # 5
        )
    def forward(self, x):
        return self.classifier(self.features(x))

# ---------------- Utils ----------------
def pad_or_crop(a, T):
    c, t = a.shape
    if t < T:
        pad = np.zeros((c, T - t), dtype=a.dtype)
        a = np.concatenate([a, pad], axis=1)
    elif t > T:
        a = a[:, :T]
    return a

def try_find_processed_npy(wav_path, processed_root):
    stem = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(wav_path))[0]
    hits = glob.glob(os.path.join(processed_root, "**", f"{stem}*.npy"), recursive=True)
    return hits[0] if hits else None

def load_mfcc_stats(stats_path):
    if stats_path and os.path.exists(stats_path):
        with open(stats_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            js = json.load(f)
        mean = np.asarray(js.get("mean"), dtype=np.float32)
        std  = np.asarray(js.get("std"),  dtype=np.float32)
        if mean.shape == (13,) and std.shape == (13,) and np.all(std > 0):
            return mean, std
    return None, None

def librosa_mfcc_13(wav_path, sr_target=16000, n_fft=1024, hop_length=160, win_length=None, center=True, htk=False, norm='ortho'):
    y, sr = librosa.load(wav_path, sr=sr_target, mono=True)
    mfcc = librosa.feature.mfcc(
        y=y, sr=sr, n_mfcc=13,
        n_fft=n_fft, hop_length=hop_length, win_length=win_length,
        center=center, htk=htk, norm=norm
    )
    return mfcc.astype(np.float32)

def load_label_map_any(path, fallback=None):
    cls = None
    if path and os.path.exists(path):
        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            lm = json.load(f)
        if isinstance(lm, list) and all(isinstance(x, str) for x in lm):
            cls = lm
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and "classes" in lm and isinstance(lm["classes"], list):
            if all(isinstance(x, str) for x in lm["classes"]):
                cls = lm["classes"]
            elif all(isinstance(x, dict) and "name" in x for x in lm["classes"]):
                cls = [x["name"] for x in lm["classes"]]
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and all(isinstance(k,str) for k in lm.keys()) and all(isinstance(v,int) for v in lm.values()):
            size = max(lm.values())+1
            cls = [None]*size
            for name, idx in lm.items():
                if 0 <= idx < size: cls[idx] = name
            if any(x is None for x in cls):
                cls = [k for k,_ in sorted(lm.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1])]
        elif isinstance(lm, dict) and all(isinstance(v,str) for v in lm.values()):
            try:
                kv = [(int(k),v) for k,v in lm.items()]
                kv.sort(key=lambda x:x[0]); cls = [v for _,v in kv]
            except Exception:
                pass
    return cls if cls is not None else (fallback or [])

def window_rms(mfcc_win):
    return float(np.sqrt(np.mean(mfcc_win**2)))

# ---------------- Inference over multiple windows ----------------
def infer_multi_windows(mfcc, model, frames, device, multi, strategy, focus, focus_k):
    T = frames
    total = mfcc.shape[1]
    if total <= T or multi <= 1:
        win = pad_or_crop(mfcc, T)[None, None, ...]
        with torch.no_grad():
            logits = model(torch.from_numpy(win).to(device))
            probs = F.softmax(logits, dim=1).cpu().numpy()[0]
        return probs

    starts = np.linspace(0, max(total - T, 0), num=multi, dtype=int)
    windows = []
    for s in starts:
        sub = mfcc[:, s:s+T]
        sub = pad_or_crop(sub, T)
        windows.append(sub)

    if focus == "loudest":
        scores = [window_rms(w) for w in windows]
        keep_idx = np.argsort(scores)[-min(focus_k, len(windows)):]
        windows = [windows[i] for i in keep_idx]

    agg_logits = None
    agg_probs  = None
    for sub in windows:
        x = sub[None, None, ...]
        with torch.no_grad():
            logits = model(torch.from_numpy(x).to(device))
            probs  = F.softmax(logits, dim=1)

        if strategy in ("logit_mean", "logit-mean"):
            agg_logits = probs.new_zeros_like(logits) if agg_logits is None else agg_logits
            agg_logits += logits
        elif strategy == "max":
            agg_probs = probs if agg_probs is None else torch.maximum(agg_probs, probs)
        else:  # mean
            agg_probs = probs if agg_probs is None else agg_probs + probs

    if strategy in ("logit_mean", "logit-mean"):
        probs = F.softmax(agg_logits / len(windows), dim=1).cpu().numpy()[0]
    elif strategy == "max":
        probs = agg_probs.cpu().numpy()[0]
    else:
        probs = (agg_probs / len(windows)).cpu().numpy()[0]
    return probs

# ---------------- Main ----------------
def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Infer frog species from a WAV file.")
    ap.add_argument("--wav", required=True, help="Path to audio .wav")
    ap.add_argument("--npy", help="(Optional) Path to processed MFCC .npy (13,T or 13,T,1)")
    ap.add_argument("--processed-only", action="store_true", help="Require a processed .npy; error if none found")
    ap.add_argument("--model", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","frog_cnn_best.pt"))
    ap.add_argument("--label-map", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","label_map.json"))
    ap.add_argument("--frames", type=int, default=100)
    ap.add_argument("--topk", type=int, default=5)
    ap.add_argument("--uncertain-threshold", type=float, default=None)
    ap.add_argument("--normalize", action="store_true", help="(fallback librosa path) per-coef z-norm per clip")
    ap.add_argument("--multi", type=int, default=12, help="# windows to evaluate")
    ap.add_argument("--strategy", choices=["mean","max","logit_mean","logit-mean"], default="mean")
    ap.add_argument("--focus", choices=["none","loudest"], default="none", help="Keep only loudest windows (RMS)")
    ap.add_argument("--focus-k", type=int, default=6, help="# windows to keep if --focus loudest")
    ap.add_argument("--device", default="cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
    ap.add_argument("--processed-root", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","data","processed"))
    ap.add_argument("--mfcc-stats", default=os.path.join("FrogSoundProject","models","mfcc_stats.json"))
    args = ap.parse_args()

    print("=== Frog Inference ===")
    print(f"WAV:      {args.wav}")
    print(f"Model:    {args.model}")
    print(f"Labels:   {args.label_map}")
    print(f"Frames:   {args.frames} | TopK: {args.topk}")

    ckpt = torch.load(args.model, map_location="cpu")
    classes = None
    target_frames = args.frames
    state_dict = None

    if isinstance(ckpt, dict):
        if "classes" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["classes"], list):
            classes = ckpt["classes"]; print(f"Loaded {len(classes)} classes from checkpoint.")
        if "frames" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["frames"], int):
            target_frames = ckpt["frames"]; print(f"Using target frames from checkpoint: {target_frames}")
        if "model_state" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["model_state"], dict):
            state_dict = ckpt["model_state"]
        elif "state_dict" in ckpt and isinstance(ckpt["state_dict"], dict):
            state_dict = ckpt["state_dict"]

    if classes is None:
        classes = load_label_map_any(args.label_map)
        print(f"Loaded {len(classes)} classes from label_map.json")
    n_classes = len(classes)

    model = FrogCNN(n_classes=n_classes)
    if state_dict is None:
        if isinstance(ckpt, dict):
            tensor_only = {k:v for k,v in ckpt.items() if isinstance(v, torch.Tensor)}
            state_dict = tensor_only if tensor_only else ckpt
        else:
            state_dict = ckpt
    missing, unexpected = model.load_state_dict(state_dict, strict=False)
    if missing or unexpected:
        print(f"⚠️ load_state_dict warnings — missing: {list(missing)}, unexpected: {list(unexpected)}")

    device = torch.device(args.device); model.to(device); model.eval()

    # -------- Feature source --------
    if args.npy:
        if not os.path.exists(args.npy):
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"--npy not found: {args.npy}")
        a = np.load(args.npy).astype(np.float32)
        if a.ndim == 3: a = a.squeeze(-1)
        if a.shape[0] != 13:
            raise ValueError(f"--npy has unexpected shape {a.shape}, expected (13,T) or (13,T,1)")
        mfcc = a
        feature_src = "Processed (.npy, explicit)"
    else:
        npy_match = try_find_processed_npy(args.wav, args.processed_root)
        if npy_match and os.path.exists(npy_match):
            a = np.load(npy_match).astype(np.float32)
            if a.ndim == 3: a = a.squeeze(-1)
            if a.shape[0] != 13:
                raise ValueError(f"Processed file has unexpected shape: {a.shape}")
            mfcc = a
            feature_src = "Processed (.npy, auto)"
        else:
            if args.processed_only:
                raise FileNotFoundError("No matching processed .npy found and --processed-only set.")
            mfcc = librosa_mfcc_13(args.wav, sr_target=16000, n_fft=1024, hop_length=160)
            if args.normalize:
                mfcc = (mfcc - mfcc.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)) / (mfcc.std(axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-6)
            mean, std = load_mfcc_stats(args.mfcc_stats)
            if mean is not None and std is not None:
                mfcc = (mfcc - mean[:,None]) / (std[:,None] + 1e-6)
            feature_src = "Librosa MFCC (fallback)"

    print(f"Feature source: {feature_src}")

    # -------- Inference --------
    probs = infer_multi_windows(
        mfcc, model, target_frames, device,
        multi=max(1, args.multi), strategy=args.strategy,
        focus=args.focus, focus_k=args.focus_k
    )

    topk = min(args.topk, n_classes)
    idxs = np.argsort(-probs)[:topk]
    print("\nTop predictions:")
    for i in idxs:
        print(f"  {classes[i]:<32} {probs[i]*100:6.2f}%")

    if args.uncertain_threshold is not None:
        top = probs[idxs[0]] * 100.0
        if top < args.uncertain_threshold:
            print(f"\n🤔 UNCERTAIN — top confidence {top:.2f}% (< {args.uncertain_threshold:.0f}%)")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

I see the problem 👍

Your infer_wav.py is throwing SyntaxError: unterminated string literal (detected at line 2) — that means the file itself has gotten corrupted somehow. Specifically, the @' at line 2 is not valid Python. That looks like a leftover from a PowerShell here-string (PowerShell uses @'…'@ for multi-line strings).

So instead of Python code, your file has some PowerShell junk at the top. That explains both:

why --npy and --focus aren’t recognized (they never existed in your original parser),

and why --help now fails instantly before even showing argparse.

🔧 How to Fix

Open FrogSoundProject\src\infer_wav.py in VS Code (or any editor).

At the very top, remove any stray lines like:

@'


or anything else that doesn’t belong in Python.
The file should start with something like:

import argparse
import torch
import numpy as np
...


not with PowerShell syntax.

Save the file.

Run again:

python FrogSoundProject\src\infer_wav.py --help


You should now see the proper argparse options again (only --wav, --model, --label-map, --frames, --multi, etc.).

Thought for 20s

Nice — your infer_wav.py is back in shape and it’s picking up the processed .npy now (that’s the most reliable path). The bad ID for acris_blanchardi is almost certainly coming from how we window/aggregate the clip at inference, not from mislabeled classes (you’re loading the 65-class list from the checkpoint correctly).

Here’s how to get a much more stable answer on that same file, plus why each tweak helps.

What’s happening (quick)

Your clip has ~1834 MFCC frames (from your earlier debug output). With --frames 100, there are ~18 non-overlapping windows in the audio.

Using --multi 12 spreads only twelve evenly-spaced (non-overlapping) crops across the whole file. You can easily miss the strongest calls or overweight quiet/noisy parts.

Averaging probs (--strategy mean) dilutes “peaky” windows. Logit-space averaging is usually better.

Use these settings (copy/paste)

Run all three and compare — they’re fast:

A) Cover more of the file + better aggregation

python FrogSoundProject\src\infer_wav.py `
  --wav "C:\Users\dhruv\OneDrive\Desktop\Frog Sound Project\FrogSoundProject\data\raw\inat_frogs\acris_blanchardi\acris_blanchardi_obs302690303_s1588472.wav" `
  --npy "C:\Users\dhruv\OneDrive\Desktop\Frog Sound Project\FrogSoundProject\data\processed\acris_blanchardi\acris_blanchardi_obs302690303_s1588472.npy" `
  --model "FrogSoundProject\models\frog_cnn_best.pt" `
  --label-map "FrogSoundProject\models\label_map.json" `
  --frames 100 `
  --topk 5 `
  --multi 40 `
  --strategy logit_mean


Why: 40 windows samples far more of the clip; logit_mean (average in logit space, then softmax) preserves strong evidence that would be washed out by plain mean.

B) Same as A, but focus on the loudest parts

python FrogSoundProject\src\infer_wav.py `
  --wav "$wav" `
  --npy "$npy" `
  --model "FrogSoundProject\models\frog_cnn_best.pt" `
  --label-map "FrogSoundProject\models\label_map.json" `
  --frames 100 `
  --topk 5 `
  --multi 40 `
  --strategy logit_mean `
  --focus loudest `
  --focus-k 10


Why: Keeps the 10 loudest windows by RMS before aggregating — good for choruses, reduces background bias.

C) Quick sanity check (single centered crop)

python FrogSoundProject\src\infer_wav.py `
  --wav "$wav" `
  --npy "$npy" `
  --model "FrogSoundProject\models\frog_cnn_best.pt" `
  --label-map "FrogSoundProject\models\label_map.json" `
  --frames 100 `
  --topk 5 `
  --multi 1 `
  --strategy mean


Why: Shows what one “typical” 100-frame crop thinks. If this is way off while A or B looks good, the clip is heterogeneous and multi-window is required.

Small gotchas to avoid

Don’t use --normalize when you give --npy (processed MFCCs). Normalizing twice can hurt.

Prefer the processed path (--npy or auto-detected under data\processed) so we exactly match training input scaling.

If your clip was in the train split, the model still won’t necessarily “memorize” it because we trained with balanced sampling and a compact CNN; that’s okay — inference should succeed without memorization.

If it’s still wrong

We can add two optional filters that often help field results:

Geography/species filter: pass a whitelist (e.g., only North American species).

Temporal focus: enable a light overlap between windows (currently windows are evenly spaced).

If you want, I’ll patch infer_wav.py to add:

--overlap 0.5 (50% stride within each evenly-spaced anchor)

--whitelist path\to\species.txt (only score those classes; renormalize the softmax)

Say the word and I’ll drop a ready-to-run script update.

Thanks for the detailed runs — this explains the behavior.

What’s going on

You’re passing --npy which loads the processed feature for that clip. That file (...s1588472.npy) is already (13, 100) frames.

When the feature is exactly 100 frames, there’s only one window to score. So --multi, --strategy, and --focus don’t change anything — you’ll see the same probabilities every time (which is exactly what you observed).

That’s why your three commands (multi=40, focus loudest, and multi=1) all returned the same ranking.

Do this to really test multi-window inference on the full audio

Use the raw WAV path (no --npy) and feed the global MFCC stats you computed:

python FrogSoundProject\src\infer_wav.py `
  --wav "C:\Users\dhruv\OneDrive\Desktop\Frog Sound Project\FrogSoundProject\data\raw\inat_frogs\acris_blanchardi\acris_blanchardi_obs302690303_s1588472.wav" `
  --model "FrogSoundProject\models\frog_cnn_best.pt" `
  --label-map "FrogSoundProject\models\label_map.json" `
  --frames 100 `
  --topk 5 `
  --multi 60 `
  --strategy logit_mean `
  --focus loudest `
  --focus-k 15 `
  --mfcc-stats "FrogSoundProject\models\mfcc_stats.json"

Why these flags?

No --npy → we compute MFCCs from the whole WAV and can truly slide/slice the clip.

--mfcc-stats → matches the global scaling you built; usually improves confidence vs per-clip z-norm.

--multi 60 + --focus loudest --focus-k 15 → sample the clip densely, then keep the loudest parts (reduces background).

--strategy logit_mean → aggregate in logit space so strong evidence isn’t diluted.

Quick checks (optional but helpful)

Confirm the processed feature you pointed at is indeed (13,100):
python -c "import numpy as np; a=np.load(r'C:\Users\dhruv\OneDrive\Desktop\Frog Sound Project\FrogSoundProject\data\processed\acris_blanchardi\acris_blanchardi_obs302690303_s1588472.npy'); print(a.shape)"
If it prints (13, 100) or (13,100,1), multi-window won’t do anything when --npy is used.

If the WAV path run still looks uncertain, try per-clip normalization instead of global stats (occasionally helps): re-run the same command but remove --mfcc-stats and add --normalize.

If it’s still wrong

Two targeted upgrades (say the word and I’ll drop a patched script):

Geographic whitelist: add --whitelist path\to\species.txt to only score plausible species (e.g., North America), renormalizing the softmax. This often fixes “confident but wrong” on look-alike species from other continents.

Overlapping windows: add --overlap 0.5 so each 100-frame window strides by 50 frames; improves stability on short chirps.

TL;DR: Using --npy on a 100-frame feature locks you to a single window, so multi-window settings can’t help. Re-run on the WAV with --mfcc-stats, many windows, and logit aggregation (command above).

You hit a tiny PyTorch API slip. Tensors don’t have new_zeros_like; use torch.zeros_like(...).

Quick patch (copy-paste)

agg_logits = probs.new_zeros_like(logits) if agg_logits is None else agg_logits

agg_logits = torch.zeros_like(logits) if agg_logits is None else agg_logits
